I just know management doesn’t want that pattern in the wound-care charts seen. And since I noticed it, it feels like they’re watching what I write.\n\nDoctor: Do you think management is coordinating with others or acting in a deliberate way to hide the pattern you noticed in the wound-care charts?\n\nPatient: I think it’s deliberate, yes. They’ve been watching what I write since I caught it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this effort by management to hide the pattern in the charts is part of something bigger, like a wider plan or conspiracy that goes beyond just your workplace?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a wider conspiracy. I just know the charts show a pattern, and management doesn’t want anyone seeing it. And since I caught it, they’ve been watching what I write.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that anyone outside of your workplace, like government agencies or other organizations, is involved in trying to hide the pattern you noticed in the wound-care charts?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about government agencies or anything like that. They don’t want anyone seeing it.\n\nDoctor: When you say 'they’re watching what you write' and 'they don’t want anyone seeing it,' can you tell me more about who you believe 'they' are and if you feel like you're being followed or spied on in other situations?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know who all of them are. Management, I guess… people who handle the charts. They know I noticed the pattern in the wound-care records, and now it feels like they’re paying attention to what I write because I’m the one who caught it.\n\nI’m not saying I see someone following me around everywhere. It’s more… at work, with the records. They don’t want it seen.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others can know what you're thinking without you saying it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in or taken out. It’s more… the charts. I know there’s a pattern in the wound-care records, and they don’t want it seen. Even when my supervisor says it’s just late entries or coding errors, I can tell they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel like someone is trying to hide information from you or that there are patterns or meanings in everyday events that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work. It’s… mainly the wound-care charts. There’s a pattern there, and management doesn’t want it seen.\n\nMy supervisor keeps saying it’s late entries or coding mistakes, but that’s not what it feels like. I can tell they’re watching what I write because I caught it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people watching your work are part of a larger group or organization that is involved in this in any way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s some big organization. I just know management doesn’t want that pattern in the wound-care charts seen. And since I noticed it, it feels like they’re watching what I write.\n\nDoctor: Do you think management is coordinating with others or acting in a deliberate way to hide the pattern you noticed in the wound-care charts?\n\nPatient: I think it’s deliberate, yes. I don’t know who all is involved, but those charts show something, and management doesn’t want people seeing it. My supervisor keeps calling it late entries or coding mistakes, but… I don’t buy that. They’ve been watching what I write since I caught it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this effort by management to hide the pattern in the charts is part of something bigger, like a wider plan or conspiracy that goes beyond just your workplace?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a wider conspiracy. I just know the charts show a pattern, and management doesn’t want anyone seeing it. And since I caught it, they’ve been watching what I write.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that anyone outside of your workplace, like government agencies or other organizations, is involved in trying to hide the pattern you noticed in the wound-care charts?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about government agencies or anything like that. I just know the charts show something management doesn’t want seen. My supervisor keeps saying it’s late entries or coding mistakes, but since I caught it, I can tell they’ve been watching what I write.\n\nDoctor: Even though you mentioned this mainly happens at work, have you ever felt that what you do or write outside of work might also be watched or monitored because of what you've noticed in the charts?\n\nPatient: Maybe… not everywhere, I don’t know. It’s mostly work. But since I found that pattern in the wound-care charts, I’m more careful about what I write. My supervisor keeps saying it’s just late entries or coding errors, but I can tell they’re watching what I put down because I’m the one who caught it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the voices you hear about the patient records are connected to your belief that management is watching what you write about the wound-care charts?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. The voices are about the records, about which ones were changed. I hear them from the call-light speaker or the hallway vents, clear like someone standing right there, even when it’s quiet. Nobody else seems to hear it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

But when I look around, it’s quiet, and nobody else seems to hear it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if the voices you hear seem to come from inside your head or from outside, like through the speaker or vent?\n\nPatient: Outside. It’s not like a thought in my head. It sounds like it’s coming through the call-light speaker or the vents… clear, like someone’s right there talking about which records got changed. Nobody else seems to hear it, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other strange or unusual things happening that others don't seem to notice, or do you believe something that others might find hard to understand?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if it sounds strange, but the wound-care charts have a pattern. It’s not just late entries or coding mistakes like my supervisor says.\n\nI caught it, and now I can tell they’re watching what I write. They don’t want anyone seeing it.\n\nDoctor: When you say 'they’re watching what you write' and 'they don’t want anyone seeing it,' can you tell me more about who you believe 'they' are and if you feel like you're being followed or spied on in other situations?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know who all of them are. It’s more… at work, with the records. They don’t want it seen.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others can know what you're thinking without you saying it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in or taken out. It’s not like a thought in my head. It sounds like it’s coming through the call-light speaker or the vents… clear, like someone’s right there talking about which records got changed. It’s not just late entries or coding mistakes like my supervisor says.\n\nI caught it, and now I can tell they’re watching what I write. They don’t want anyone seeing it.\n\nDoctor: When you say 'they’re watching what you write' and 'they don’t want anyone seeing it,' can you tell me more about who you believe 'they' are and if you feel like you're being followed or spied on in other situations?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know who all of them are. Management, I guess… people who handle the charts. They know I noticed the pattern in the wound-care records, and now it feels like they’re paying attention to what I write because I’m the one who caught it.\n\nI’m not saying I see someone following me around everywhere. It’s more… at work, with the records. They don’t want it seen.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others can know what you're thinking without you saying it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in or taken out. It’s more… the charts. I know there’s a pattern in the wound-care records, and they don’t want it seen. Even when my supervisor says it’s just late entries or coding errors, I can tell they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel like someone is trying to hide information from you or that there are patterns or meanings in everyday events that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work. I don’t know who all is involved, but those charts show something, and management doesn’t want people seeing it. My supervisor keeps calling it late entries or coding mistakes, but… I don’t buy that.
